# vLLM efficiency points (2026-11-11)

Source data for `DECODE_EFFICIENCY` and `PREFILL_EFFICIENCY` in `src/engines/constants.ts`
(spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-11-calibration-sparse-attention-design.md`, P1). Each
constant is the **median** of its accepted points (user decision 2026-11-11, replacing the spec's
earlier "pessimistic end" rule). The median is the sorted value at index floor(0.5 x (n - 1)),
the same rule as the percentiles below.

## Distribution of accepted points

p = sorted value at index floor(p x (n - 1)).

| Phase                                            | n   | min   | p5    | p10   | p25   | median |
| ------------------------------------------------ | --- | ----- | ----- | ----- | ----- | ------ |
| Decode (measured / engine)                       | 512 | 0.126 | 0.270 | 0.315 | 0.380 | 0.470  |
| Prefill, used: closed-loop queueing, mean TTFT   | 62  | 0.101 | 0.143 | 0.239 | 0.344 | 0.469  |
| Prefill, as ruled: burst (c + 1) / 2, mean TTFT  | 62  | 0.219 | 0.311 | 0.520 | 0.747 | 1.020  |
| Prefill, alternative: no correction, median TTFT | 62  | 0.118 | 0.186 | 0.213 | 0.361 | 0.494  |

**Deviation from the ruled queueing correction.** The ruled correction (all c prompts arrive
together, factor (c + 1) / 2) gives a prefill median of 1.020, which is above 1, and 33 of 62
rows above 1. Two pieces of evidence say its premise does not fit this harness:
- **Impossible utilisation.** On H200 FP8 TP1 at 8k, it implies 2.47 x the engine's 0.45 MFU,
  which is 111% of the card's dense peak.
- **The harness is a closed loop.** It runs `--num-prompts 10c --max-concurrency c`, so only the
  first c of 10c prompts arrive together and the rest arrive staggered. The predicted mean
  factor is 0.9 + 0.1 x (c + 1) / 2, which is 1.15 at c4. Mean / median TTFT on that H200 row is
  1344.8 / 1165.9 = 1.15.

The constant therefore uses the closed-loop factor against mean TTFT (median 0.469). The
uncorrected engine prefill against median TTFT gives 0.494. The lower of the two is taken, per the
standing "take the safer one" rule. With either, the highest FP8 ratio is 1.14, which is 51% of
peak.

## Sources

- **SemiAnalysis InferenceX**, public API `https://inferencex.semianalysis.com/api/v1/benchmarks?model=<name>`
  (`gpt-oss-120b`, `Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct-FP8`), fetched 2026-11-11. The API serves the latest run
  per configuration; every row below links its GitHub Actions run.
  - Llama 3.3 70B configs and scripts: InferenceX commit
    [9596d23](https://github.com/SemiAnalysisAI/InferenceX/tree/9596d23d74e9e1e4f4bcdb27572c87cafb746a02)
    (`.github/workflows/70b-tmpl.yml`, `benchmarks/70b_*`). The record's `image` is null; the version
    is derived from that commit: `vllm/vllm-openai:v0.10.2` (NVIDIA), ROCm `vllm_0.10.1` (AMD).
  - gpt-oss-120b configs and scripts: InferenceX commit
    [a1084c2](https://github.com/SemiAnalysisAI/InferenceX/tree/a1084c247fd4112f2ced940649ce0362882e69e5)
    (`.github/configs/nvidia-master.yaml`, `benchmarks/single_node/gptoss_fp4_*.sh`). The image is stated per row.
- **AMD**, Performance Results with ROCm Software,
  https://www.amd.com/en/developer/resources/rocm-hub/dev-ai/performance-results.html: vLLM 0.11.2
  (`rocm/vllm:rocm7.0.0_vllm_0.11.2_20251210`), 8x MI300X, TP8, FP8 weights + FP8 KV, offline latency.

## Method

- The engine runs at each matched configuration: `calculateInferenceVRAM`, then
  `calculateMultiGPUVRAM('tensor-parallel')`, then `estimatePerformance`, using database rows.
  GPU map: h100 is `nvidia-h100-80gb-sxm`, h200 `nvidia-h200-141gb`, b200 `nvidia-b200-192gb`,
  mi300x / mi325x / mi355x `amd-*`.
- Formats: Llama fp8 is `fp8`, B200 fp4 is `nvfp4` (nvidia/Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct-FP4), MI355X fp4 is
  `mxfp4` (amd/Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct-MXFP4-Preview), all with FP8 KV. gpt-oss is `mxfp4`, with FP8 KV
  on B200 and BF16 KV elsewhere (per the scripts).
- InferenceX random lengths are uniform in [0.8 L, L] (bench_serving `sample_uniform`,
  `RANDOM_RANGE_RATIO=0.8`), so the mean prompt is 0.9 ISL.
- InferenceX rows kept: `framework == vllm`, single node, no disagg, no speculative decoding,
  `single_turn`, no offload, EP 1, no DP attention. Rows from TensorRT-LLM, SGLang, ATOM, Dynamo,
  llm-d and disaggregated vLLM are not used.
- **Decode:** efficiency = engine ITL / measured mean ITL. Engine ITL = batch / tok/s, with batch =
  concurrency, at the mean decode context 0.9 ISL + 0.45 OSL. Mean ITL equals mean TPOT on the
  accepted rows (median ratio 1.000), and recomputing on TPOT leaves the minimum unchanged.
  - Accepted: ISL 1024.
  - Rejected: ISL 8192. About 8 prompt tokens per output token share the steps, so the ITL
    measures prefill.
  - AMD rows: step = latency / 2048 at context input + 1024. Accepted: input 128 (prefill under 2%
    of latency). Rejected: input 2048 (the B x 2048 prefill is in the latency).
- **Prefill:** efficiency = engine prefill / measured mean TTFT. The engine prefill is computed for
  one mean prompt (0.9 ISL). One rule set applies to every row:
  - (a) Reject if the engine prefill is under 50 ms: fixed request latency dominates. This
    replaces the earlier ISL-1024 rule but does not cover the same rows. It rejects 82 ISL-1024 and
    15 ISL-8192 rows at c <= 4, and accepts 22 ISL-1024 rows.
  - (b) Queueing at concurrency c: the engine side is engine prefill x (0.9 + 0.1 x (c + 1) / 2).
    That is the closed-loop mean: the first c of 10c prompts arrive together under FIFO chunked
    prefill, and the rest arrive alone. The ruled burst factor (c + 1) / 2 is shown alongside;
    see the deviation note above.
  - Concurrency upper bound: c <= 4.
  - No AMD prefill points: the source has no TTFT.

`https://github.com/SemiAnalysisAI/InferenceX/actions/runs/<id>`.
